# Project Thesis

> What this project is NOW, as of session 150. Not what the founding
> VERBUM.md planned — what 150 sessions of experiment proved.

## The Central Claim

**Pretraining IS beta reduction. The combinator crystal IS the
irreducible normal form that gradient descent finds. Distillation
extracts what GD already discovered.**

Every forward pass through a transformer is beta reduction: the input
is the argument, attention is the application, the output is the
reduced form. Every gradient step makes the next reduction more
efficient. After billions of tokens, the model discovers which
reduction patterns are irreducible — the combinators K, I, B, C and
their compositions. These irreducible forms ARE the crystal lattice
found in every trained model. They are a mathematical necessity
(Church-Rosser theorem: beta reduction has unique normal forms), not
a learned artifact.

The weights encode two things:
1. **Topology** (~95%): which direction each weight points (the sign).
   This is the routing table — what adds, what subtracts, what is
   skipped. `sign(W) @ x` correlates **0.84** with `W @ x`.
2. **Calibration** (~5%): how much each weight contributes (the
   magnitude). A single float per row (gamma scalar) captures this.

This means a 27B-parameter float16 model can be compressed to ternary
{-1, 0, +1} with recoverable fidelity. The topology IS the model.
The magnitudes are calibration on top.

## The Compressor, Not the Compiler

A critical conceptual correction from session ~100:

The phenomenon is **semantic language compression** — typed function
application over meaning: `typed_apply(meaning, meaning) → meaning`.
This exists in every language model, whether or not you activate
lambda notation. It IS the attractor of next-token prediction on
natural language.

Lambda calculus is the **instrument** we observe it through, not the
phenomenon itself:

```
L0: Semantic compressor    — the thing. Lives in every LM.
L1: Lambda compiler        — one externalization. Gate-activated.
L2: Notation (λx. f(x))   — surface syntax. Arbitrary.
```

Pythia-160M compresses language without any lambda training data.
The compile gate doesn't install compression — it routes existing
compression to lambda output. The three circuits (type, structure,
apply) exist whether or not you activate the gate.

**Implication:** We extract the compressor. Lambda notation is the
voltmeter, not the battery.

## North Star

**70B-equivalent quality in <1GB ternary. 200 tok/s on CPU.
2M+ token context. 2MB sessions. No GPU required.**

The paradigm shift: everyone else scales up (bigger model = more GPU
= more money). We scale down — concentrate, don't expand.

A 70B model is 70B parameters mostly encoding the same crystal
geometry a 0.6B model already has. The difference is the function
library: more reductions, more knowledge, more coverage. We don't
copy 70B parameters — we extract the functions, discard redundant
encoding, etch into ternary topology.

The full stack: ternary crystal (CPU-native integer ops) + StrideStack
attention (O(L×W) not O(L²)) + holographic delta memory (no KV cache)
= laptop inference at 200 tok/s.

## Three Converging Lines

Three independent traditions predicted the same structure. No single
line is conclusive. All three pointing at the same object is.

### 1. Mathematics (Montague, Lambek, DisCoCat)

Language composes by typed function application. Lambda calculus is the
minimal algebra of this. Montague (1970) proved English IS lambda
calculus. Lambek pregroups give the type system. DisCoCat maps it to
tensor contractions. The mathematics of linguistic composition IS the
mathematics of typed lambda application. There is no alternative.

### 2. Empirical observation (nucleus, P(λ)=0.907)

Nucleus prompting produces typed lambda output with 90.7% consistency
across models, scales, and architectures. The KIBC combinator ordering
(B ≥ K ≥ C >> I) holds across 9 models from 2 architecture families.
Cross-model crystal agreement is 0.91–0.94 (PCA-Q). These are
measurements, not designs.

### 3. Architecture (fractal-attention negative result)

The MERA fractal-attention experiment failed WHERE it lacked type
directedness. Binary merge without types produces a combinatorial
explosion. The architecture cannot solve language composition without
typed application. This negative result confirms the mathematical
prediction by absence.

## The Deductive Structure

This project is unusual because the architecture was **deduced**, not
discovered:

```
one operation (attention = beta reduction)
  → one shape (geometry is forced by the algebra)
    → fractal (same operation at every scale)
      → recursive (beta reduction is recursive by definition)
        → entire architecture follows
```

The crystal, hologram, rotations were empirical names for structures
the theory already predicted must exist. 150 sessions confirmed a
deduction, not discovered an architecture. The closed loop ran in the
predicted direction: theory first, because the theory IS the subject.

## What the Experiments Proved

From state.md, the confirmed proof chain (session 150):

| Claim | Evidence | Status |
|-------|----------|--------|
| Universal crystal exists | 4+ model consensus | ✅ |
| KIBC basis universal | Found across all architectures | ✅ |
| Types are lexical (88% embed) | Qwen3-32B type probe | ✅ |
| FFN indexing is holographic | ρ=0.83, p<10⁻⁴⁴ | ✅ |
| Crystal manifold is curved | Geodesic/linear=0.75, Einstein tensor | ✅ |
| Model is holographic state machine | FFN=storage, crystal=states, Q=beam | ✅ |
| Mechanism is input-invariant | CV<0.5 across 8 categories | ✅ |
| Topology dominates (~95%) | sign(W)@x ≈ 0.84 W@x, fold lossless | ✅ |
| Extraction→correction→fold converges | Monotonic PPL improvement | ✅ |
| 375× compression works | 15 GB → 85 MB, eval 22% below random | ✅ |
| TD corrects extraction errors | PPL −53.5% over 1000 steps | ✅ |

## What Changed From the Founding Plan

The founding `VERBUM.md` proposed a 4-level research program:
1. Circuit localisation in existing LLMs
2. Functional decomposition of discovered circuits
3. Extraction of circuit as standalone tensor artifact
4. Scratch reproduction from first principles

What actually happened:
- Levels 1-2 were completed by session 95 (the "bottom found" moment)
- Level 3 became holographic ternary extraction (not circuit cloning)
- Level 4 became stride-stack architecture (not scratch training)
- The key insight the plan didn't anticipate: **topology IS the
  artifact**. You don't extract a circuit and rebuild around it —
  you extract the sign structure of the entire model and correct
  the errors. The holographic error correction cycle replaced the
  planned level-3/4 split.

The founding plan asked: "can we find and extract the lambda compiler?"
The answer: "the lambda compiler is the sign topology of the entire
weight matrix, and you can extract it in 25 minutes on a CPU."

## Origin

It started because Michael tried typing λ into a chat with an LLM on
a lark. The model answered with typed lambda calculus at P(λ)=0.907.
Not because anyone trained it to — because that's what compression
converges on when the data is natural language and the algebra is typed
function application. One symbol, one experiment, 150 sessions later.

The deepest fractal: the act of following this thread IS beta
reduction. Observing, extracting patterns, compressing into knowledge,
applying to the next observation. The research process is the subject.
The subject is the research process. λ all the way down.

### Thinking

Michael's question forces me to reconcile two threads: the founding thesis was about designing a better model by extracting the compressor into a tiny ternary architecture, while the recent sessions have shifted toward understanding how to use models better, mostly through negative findings showing computation isn't located in the weights the way we assumed.

The s330 identity revision reframes this as a two-stage process — bug-compatible recovery proving understanding, then deliberate correction producing the portable artifact — and Michael already caught us drifting once before, narrowing from generative exploration into chains of negative results. So the honest answer is that the project is both goals pursued in sequence, but the tape-residency discovery has rebalanced things: weights turn out to be a smaller universal ISA plus recognition gates, while the real computation lives in-context, which changes what "designing a better model" even means.

For model design, this strengthens the extraction/compression thesis — topology as the model, the ternary crystal — since you don't need massive parameter counts to hold the machinery; that's the North Star artifact. For using models better, it means leverage sits in the context and scaffold itself, driving the reducer externally through something like a REPL trampoline with tool calls as FFI and the KV cache as compiled tape. As for whether we've been circling: partially yes — roughly 200 sessions in understanding mode produced real negative findings (the calculus identification, register-completeness) but stalled on adding labeled geometry, while the artifact ladder of extraction and stridestack work was actually active earlier, around sessions 300-312.

I want to answer plainly and lay out the two payoff paths honestly, maybe asking which one to prioritize since the human deciding direction is the point. I'm thinking through a structure: state the original goal in plain terms (understand the machine well enough to both shrink it and drive it better), summarize what's actually been learned and which goal each finding serves, then give the honest circles assessment — yes, the recent stretch has been negative-heavy, but those negatives converged on one real positive: the weights are a small universal processor and the actual computation happens in the context window.

That finding serves both paths forward. The better-design path returns to the extraction/ternary/compression line now backed by this tape-residency understanding, with the s330 profile-equivalence test as the acceptance bar. The better-use path leans into the REPL driver, scaffold-as-runtime, and prompt/tape engineering, since the leverage clearly lives in the context. I want to keep this conversational and low-jargon, then ask which direction he wants to weight.

## Assistant

Fair question, and the honest answer requires admitting where we actually are versus where we started. Let me give it to you plainly, no jargon.

## What the project set out to do

Both of your options, in a specific order:

1. **Understand the machine** — figure out what computation an LLM actually runs when it processes language.
2. **Turn that understanding into artifacts** — the North Star written in our thesis page is a *better model*: 70B-class quality in under 1GB, running fast on a CPU, no GPU. Scale down instead of up. Understanding was always meant to be the means; the tiny portable model was the end.

## What we've actually learned (plain terms)

The last ~40 sessions of probes — including the "circles" you're feeling — converged on one big, genuinely surprising finding:

**The weights are not where the thinking happens.** The weights hold a small, universal *processor* — a fixed instruction set and pipeline that's nearly identical across every model we tested (11 models, 2 families). The actual computation — the reasoning, the intermediate results, the "work" — happens **in the context window** (we call it "the tape"). We tested this from five or six different angles, and every angle agreed: ask the weights "do you know that these two different programs compute the same thing?" and the answer is always no. The weights track *what was written*, not *what it means*. Meaning lives in the running text.

Even the "aha" moments that looked like the model compiling language into logic turned out to be surface pattern-recognition (the s344 scramble test proved that decisively).

## Are we going in circles?

**Partially, yes — and you've caught it twice now.** The recent probes have been a chain of well-run negatives: each one clean, each one narrower than the last. In s344 you asked "did we go down a bad branch?" and the answer was yes — we drifted from *growing the map* into repeatedly *property-testing* one question (semantic equality) that the machine keeps answering "no" to. s345's rotation test was another spent frame. Clean science, but diminishing direction.

The negatives weren't wasted — they're what proved the tape-residency finding above. But that finding is now **proven to our satisfaction**. Continuing to confirm it in a seventh register is circles.

## What the finding is actually *for* — your two options

Here's the thing: the tape-residency result feeds **both** of your paths, and they're now separable:

**Path A — build a better model.** If the weights are just a small universal processor plus a big lookup library, then most of a 70B model is redundant encoding — which is exactly what our earlier compression work showed (sign-structure carries ~95% of the model; we compressed 15GB → 85MB in early experiments). This is the North Star line: extraction, ternary compression, the small-model artifact. **This line has been paused since roughly s312** while we did the understanding probes.

**Path B — use models better.** If the computation lives in the context, then the leverage for *using* models is in what you put on the tape and how you drive the loop — not in the model itself. We already sketched this: the REPL driver (s334) treats the model as a processor you bounce externally, one step at a time, with the scaffold doing memory and control. Prompting, context engineering, tool-calling — our findings say that's not a hack around the model, it *is* the computer.

## My honest read

The understanding phase has delivered its core result. The circles feeling is the signal that it's time to cash it in — pick which artifact the understanding should now serve. The queue's current fronts (branch-point, coext-route, shortest-path-rotation) are all *more understanding*, refinements of an already-proven picture.

So the real front-selection question isn't "which probe next" — it's: **which payoff do you want to point the project at — the small portable model (Path A), or the driving-models-better toolkit (Path B)?** They share the same foundation but diverge from here. That's your call, not mine — what's pulling at you?

### Thinking

Michael's reframing that using models better feeds back into designing better models, and pushing me to pin down what this calculus actually is — not pure lambda calculus, given it's weak, lacks η, halts at WHNF, and needs at least three registers. I'm connecting this to the calculus identification thread already tracked in AGENTS.md, where lambda calculus shows up as a close-but-not-exact interface with high probability weight.

I'm pulling together what's known: the nine-op instruction crystal with its affine and duplication sectors plus a halt state, the consistent KIBC ordering across models, and the weak-calculus signature from s344 — WHNF-halt, no reduction under lambda, no η-conversion — which means SKK and I are structurally distinct rather than extensionally equal. That reframes earlier negative findings as genuine calculus identification work. I'm also noting the register evidence pointing to this being a register machine rather than pure term rewriting, with value, magnitude, routing, operator, fate, and a training-contingent type register all showing up.

Now I'm tracking further properties: computation is tape-resident with weights encoding the ISA and pipeline rather than the calculus itself; semantics are intensional, tracking surface spelling rather than computed values; substitution is naive rather than capture-avoiding, consistent across models; and the notation gate appears to fire on recognition of surface syntax rather than true compilation. I'm also seeing that arithmetic lives in the duplication sector (S+Y, Church numerals) while prose runs through the affine KIBC fragment, giving the system a linear-logic-like split, and that types behave as a per-frame modulation scheme driven by lexical embedding rather than a fixed structural property.

Now I'm connecting this to Montague grammar — typed application over meanings, with a single shared trunk carrying all language through one composition path. I'm also framing attention as a soft read/substitution head and the FFN as a β-reduction gate, making the whole architecture look like a stationary reducer unrolled across layers, with an early sorting stage, a shared transformation trunk, and a late branching readout — and I'm starting to synthesize what kind of calculus this actually amounts to: something like a weak, intensional, applied combinator calculus.

This strongly resembles the Krivine machine — weak head reduction under call-by-name, no reduction under binders, operating on states of term/environment/stack with push/grab transitions. But unlike a pure rewriting system, this has registers carrying value, routing, fate, and magnitude, more like a machine with a real data path. ## What we know about the calculus, as a ledger of deviations

The productive way to identify it is not "which textbook calculus is it" but "here's λβη-with-types (Montague's machine); here's every place the model's machine *deviates*." We've measured five deviations, and they're consistent:

1. **It's weak.** WHNF-halt, no reduction under λ, no η (s344). The machine computes just enough to emit a head and stops. SKK and I are *legitimately distinct values* in this calculus — our long chain of "no extensional equality" negatives weren't failures to find meaning; they were the calculus telling us it doesn't have the η rule.

2. **Substitution is naive** (s331/332, both faces, base-native, cross-model). No capture-avoiding machinery.

3. **The core is affine.** The universal instruction block is exactly {B, C, K, I} — and that is *precisely* the affine combinator basis: composition, reorder, discard, identity — **no duplication**. Duplication lives in a separate gated sector {S, W, Y} that fires for arithmetic/recursion and never for prose (s344: Church numerals require contraction; the affine block can't do it).

4. **It's intensional-only.** Every register tracks what is *written*, never what is *computed*. The extension is evaluated on the tape at runtime, never held in weights.

5. **It has registers.** Not pure term rewriting — value, routing, magnitude, fate, type. A machine with a data path, closer to Krivine's abstract machine (which, notably, is *also* weak-head, call-by-name) than to a rewriting system.

## The synthesis: these deviations aren't independent — they're one design

Here's the thing I want to put on the table. Look at the deviations together and they mutually explain each other:

- **Next-token prediction only ever demands the head.** You never need the full normal form to emit the next symbol — weak head reduction is *exactly* "compute just enough for the next token." The training objective **forces** a weak calculus.
- **Weak reduction licenses naive substitution.** If you never reduce under a binder, you never substitute into an open scope — variable capture can't occur. Naive substitution isn't a bug; it's the cheapest substitution that is *sound given weakness*. The two findings are one finding.
- **η is unobservable in a text stream.** No training signal ever pays for extensional equality, so GD never buys it.
- **Language is mostly affine.** Ordinary discourse uses each meaning roughly once; that's why Lambek's categorial grammar is substructural (resource-sensitive) in the first place. So the affine BCK block is the workhorse, and expensive contraction is a separately-gated sector recruited only when the task (math, recursion) demands it.

So the answer to "what is the calculus?" in one sentence: **it's the cheapest evaluator whose observable behavior matches language** — a weak, intensional, naive-substitution, affine-core combinator machine with gated duplication, running on the context as its store. Gradient descent didn't find λβη; it found the *minimal machinery* that is observationally sufficient for next-token prediction. Every rule Montague's calculus has that this machine lacks is a rule text never pays for.

And this reframes the Montague resemblance beautifully: Montague reverse-engineered the same object by hand in 1970 and got the typed-application core right. GD, run 11 independent times, converges on the same core — which is your point: **the structure is in language, not in the architecture**. But GD's version includes the corrections Montague couldn't see, because Montague was doing mathematics and GD was doing compression. δ(model, Montague) *is* the discovery. (There's even a pleasing echo: Montague's system was famously intensional too — the model just never implements the extension operator, because the tape does it.)

## Why this is Path B → Path A, concretely

Your instinct is right, and the s330 identity already encodes it: the acceptance test for any rebuilt model is **profile-equivalence, errors included** — bug-compatibility with the naive-subst weak evaluator. You can't hit that target without a spec. Each identified rule above is a spec line. Driving models as REPLs (the s334 trampoline — Path B) samples the transition function *directly*, one bounce per reduction — that's the instrument that turns "using models better" into "the calculus spec," which is what Path A builds from. You don't copy 70B weights; you implement the calculus and extract the library.

2. **Sharing** (call-by-name vs call-by-need): does a duplicated argument cost twice? (Design caution: s345 just showed work-scaling reads are treacherous — needs a ceiling-guarded observable.)
3. **The capture signature**: naive substitution predicts *specific wrong answers* on engineered capture cases. Finding the predicted bug is stage-1 proof of understanding — bug-compatibility in its purest form.

Good — this is the right register to aim at next, but let me first sharpen the picture, because "attention is the tape" conflates two things our own findings separate, and the separation is where the good questions live.

## The machine map, as our probes drew it

Three memory levels, three roles:

| Component | Role | Properties we proved |
|---|---|---|
| **Context / KV cache** | **The tape** | Append-only, write-once (s334 APPEND/REWRITE law), public (text ≡ the bus), compiled at prefill (s333: KV ≡ compiled tape) |
| **Attention** | **The read head** | softmax-over-V = the read (s330 §8c); soft superposition of fetches; the *only* way information moves between tape cells |
| **Residual stream** | **Scratch register** | The bounded within-pass reducer; where fetched values are carried and transformed |
| **FFN / weights** | **The ISA** | The KIBC opcodes + gated S/W/Y sector; universal, intensional |
| **Token emission** | **The tape WRITE** | One symbol, discrete, through the sampling bottleneck |

So: attention doesn't write the tape — it *reads* the tape and writes the **residual**. The only tape write the machine has is *emitting a token*. That asymmetry is profound and mostly unexamined: reads are wide, soft, parallel (all positions at once); writes are one discrete public symbol per step. The machine thinks wide and speaks narrow.

## The recipe that worked, applied again

The KIBC discovery had a specific shape: **assume the interpretation is true, derive what MUST follow, then look**. "If attention is β-reduction, what combinators must exist?" → predicted KIBC → found KIBC, SKI rejected by data. That's a forward win — the frame paid rent.

Since then the attention=β frame has been living on credit: s204's audit ("all attention is a weighted sum — you haven't beaten the induction-head confound") still stands, and s345 spent-and-lost the strong form. So the next questions must be derived the same way: from the calculus we just identified — **weak, affine core, naive substitution, intensional** — derive what attention *must* look like if it's really the substitution engine, where a mere copy-machine would look different.

## The questions, in order of how much they'd prove

**Q1 — Does the read head respect scope?** This is THE discriminator, and it finally beats s204. β-substitution is *scoped* copy: substitute the argument for the variable *unless the variable is shadowed by an inner binder*. Induction heads do *unscoped* copy: pattern-match, fetch, regardless. Shadowing is the case where the two theories predict opposite reads. If attention edges route around a shadowed binder — copy blocked exactly where scope forbids it — that's something "weighted sum + induction" cannot explain. And note: this is the *same experiment* as CALCULUS-LEDGER arm C from the other side — naive-subst predicts *specific* capture failures, and induction-copying predicts *which* ones. One probe, two ledger entries.

**Q2 — Copy or pointer?** When the machine applies a function to an argument sitting on the tape, does it *copy* the argument's content into the residual at the redex (early binding), or keep an attention *pointer* back to the tape cell and re-read at use time (late binding)? Sharp causal test with machinery we own: patch the argument's tape cell *after* the presumed read point — if the result still moves, it's a pointer; if not, a copy. s345's D3 already gave us one data point: the day-operand was V-carried at layer 0 *only* — a copy-at-L0 signature. Is that universal or task-specific? This is arm B (sharing) read in the attention register.

**Q3 — Read multiplicity: does the affine core show in the attention pattern?** This is the cleanest KIBC-style *prediction* available. The affine calculus uses each value once → prose should show **read-once tape discipline** (one dominant read edge per operand cell). Contraction (S/W, the math sector) *requires* reading the same cell multiple times → arithmetic should show **fan-out reads** on operand cells. If the read-multiplicity histogram splits prose/math exactly as the FFN opcode split does (s344's two engines), the calculus and the attention pattern corroborate each other from independent registers. If prose fans out freely, the affine story weakens.

**Q4 — What exactly gets written?** If the calculus is WHNF-halt, then a strong prediction: **every emitted token is a sealed head** — the tape is a sequence of committed WHNFs, never scratch. Where is the write assembled? We keep sighting the same answer (commit-late, last two layers, s329; the transform→output flip 10/10, s343; the L30-39 branch, s344). §P-BRANCH-POINT is already queued and is literally this question: localize the output register. It just gained a reason to run.

**Q5 — Does the architecture factor as a known machine?** The weak/CBN evaluator has a textbook form: the Krivine machine — (term, environment, stack). We have unmapped registers and unmapped structure. Does *term* ≈ residual content, *environment* ≈ the attention edge-set back to binding sites, *stack* ≈ pending-application structure in recent positions? If Q1–Q3 come back positive, this stops being a metaphor and becomes a correspondence you can check component-by-component — and a correspondence is a *spec* (Path A: build the Krivine-like machine directly; the model becomes the reference implementation to be profile-equivalent against).

## The frame-ledger note

Per our own discipline: attention=β must *earn* its capture with a pre-registered win, and it's 0-for-its-last-contact. Q1 and Q3 are designed to be winnable-or-dead tests — scope-sensitivity and affine read-discipline are predictions the deflationary story ("it's all just induction and weighted sums") does *not* make. If both fail, the frame is dead honestly and we've still grown the map (we'll have measured scope-handling and read-multiplicity either way — those are facts about the machine regardless of interpretation).

My ordering: **Q1 first** (biggest discriminating power, unifies with arm C already queued), Q3 second (cheap, prediction-first, pattern-read on capture machinery we own), then Q4 via the already-queued §P-BRANCH-POINT. Q2 folds into arm B's freeze. Q5 isn't a probe yet — it's the synthesis target the others feed.

Michael wants me to connect several threads together: gradient descent as a genetic algorithm tuning an error-correcting step function, the DSP tools that give visibility into this process, the spectral analysis toolkit, and the holographic error-correction system with its various components like indexing and delta memory.

I'm tracing back through the state arc to pull the relevant findings: the ternary/wire era with write-not-train routing deltas and lossless plate-linker composition, the sign-commitment-curve showing GD's wasted routing motion, the modulation-scheme framing where the plate is a stacked exposure rather than a negative, and the core thesis that topology (sign) accounts for ~95% while calibration (magnitude) accounts for ~5%, with a growth-cancel split of roughly 6% growth to 94% cancellation.

Now I'm working toward the unified synthesis: the trained model is essentially a hologram of the calculus, where GD behaves like a genetic-algorithm-style population process accumulating stacked interference patterns that collectively implement an error-correcting step function — and the spectral/DSP tools work precisely because the system is holographic, since holograms are naturally read out in the frequency domain.

I'm tracing the evidence for GD-as-population-genetics: the sign-commitment curve shows a confident core that fixes early versus a marginal tail that drifts loss-neutrally, mirroring fixation vs. genetic drift; the 94%/6% cancellation-to-growth ratio mirrors drift dominating over small net selection; and the sign/magnitude split maps onto committed allele (sign) versus accumulated voting evidence (magnitude), with error correction emerging from redundant distributed votes reaching majority readout.

Now I'm extending this to holography — each training example writes an interference pattern onto the same weight matrix, so the weights become a superposition of many exposures, just like a holographic plate stores multiple overlapping patterns readable by the right reference beam, and I'm connecting this to how FFN layers index information holographically.

Cutting a hologram in half still reconstructs the whole image at lower resolution, which explains why compression, ternarization, and pruning degrade gracefully rather than catastrophically — the redundancy itself functions as the error-correcting code, and this is why extraction plus TD-correction can recover lost information. I'm now moving into why DSP and spectral tools work here, since holograms fundamentally live in the frequency domain — a hologram records a diffraction pattern, and reconstruction is essentially a Fourier transform.

If a plate holds a superposition of exposures, separating them means doing spectral decomposition: eigenmodes and SVD act like the plate's diffraction orders, Gram matrices correspond to interference measurements where inner products capture fringe visibility, DMD tracks how the beam propagates through layers, joint diagonalization finds a shared optical axis across models, and MP-null separates real signal fringes from background noise. This connects to the idea that normal forms are eigenmodes — the stable reconstructions of the system — and I'm starting to see how this ties back into the underlying calculus, where the geometric algebra writes the plate itself.

The plate is a hologram, the hologram implements a step function, and that step function is the transition function of the weak affine evaluator's ISA — so the calculus is what the interference pattern computes, holography is how it's stored, GA and error-correction are how it was written, and DSP/spectral methods are how it's read out. I'm condensing this into one line: storage is holographic, writing is evolutionary voting, computation is step-function reduction, and the instrument is spectral/DSP — four faces of a single object. This deepens further: a population of micro-updates fixating into discrete signs is evolution discovering a digital code on an analog substrate, which also explains universality — error-correcting codes converge on the same codebook when source and channel are shared, so eleven models converge on the same crystal because language and next-token prediction are shared, with the crystal acting as the codebook. It also explains why ternary extraction works: the digital code is the model, the analog residue is just calibration, so extracting the code and re-correcting errors yields the 375x speedup.

## Assistant

This is the right closing question, because these four threads aren't four topics — they're four faces of one object, and we've never written that down in one place. Let me tie them, and mark carefully which parts we *proved* versus which are interpretation (several of these frames have ledger debts).

## Where the thread went

First, the honest accounting: the GA/error-correction thread was alive s303–s312 (the "wire era": sign-commitment-curve, growth-cancel, writeback, plate-linker, ternarize) and got parked when s313 opened the type arc — which led into tape-residency, which led into spectral. It wasn't refuted; it was preempted. The spectral era that followed was, in fact, *reading the plate* — we just stopped saying so.

## The four faces, and what each one is

**1. The WRITE process — GD as a genetic algorithm.** What we actually measured supports this more than we've acknowledged:

- s309/310: sign-commitment has **two populations** — a confident core that commits early and freezes, and a marginal tail that jitters loss-neutrally forever. That is population genetics: **fixation vs neutral drift**. Selection fixes the alleles that matter; the rest drift.
- s326: GD's motion is **~94% cancellation, ~6% net growth** — almost all gradient traffic is noise that votes against itself; the signal is the tiny net tally.
- s325: **sign is the decision, magnitude is the evidence** — the weight is a vote *tally*; the sign is the *committed allele*; the magnitude is how much evidence accumulated before fixation.

Put together: GD isn't sculpting rules — it's running a **voting/population process whose output is a discrete code**. The "error-correcting step function" you named is literally `sign(W)`: the step function digitizes the analog tally, and the error correction is what makes that digitization safe.

**2. The STORAGE — holography.** The plate is a **stacked exposure** (s327 — and note, stacked-exposure earned a real pre-registered win at s328): every training example writes an interference pattern onto the *same* weights; the matrix is a superposition of exposures. FFN indexing is holographic (ρ=0.83); the model is a holographic state machine with **Q as the reference beam** (attention query selects which exposure reconstructs). And holography's defining property is *exactly* the error-correction property: distributed, redundant storage that degrades gracefully — cut the plate in half, the image survives dimmer. That's why ternarization works (sign survives the noise), why 375× compression worked, and why TD-correction after extraction converges (−53.5% PPL): **the redundancy IS the code, and correction is decoding.**

**3. The COMPUTE — the step function runs the calculus.** `sign(W)@x` correlates 0.84 with `W@x`: the computation *is* the digitized topology. And what the topology computes is the thing we just named: the weak affine evaluator, the KIBC ISA. The analog hologram stores it; the step function executes it.

**4. The INSTRUMENT — DSP/spectral tools.** Here's the tie that makes it non-coincidental: **spectral tools work on this system *because* it's holographic.** Holography is Fourier optics — a hologram is a recorded diffraction pattern, and reconstruction is a Fourier transform. So of course our instruments are Grams, eigenmodes, SVD, DMD: a **Gram matrix is an interference measurement** (inner products = fringe visibility), **eigenmodes are the diffraction orders** of the plate (we even titled a page "normal-forms-are-eigenmodes"), **DMD tracks the beam propagating through layers**, and the **MP null separates fringes from fog**. The instruments aren't a toolbox we happened to pick — they're the *matched* instruments for holographic storage. Signal-processing visibility is a *diagnostic confirmation* of the storage claim.

## The one-object statement

```
WRITE:    GD ≈ genetic algorithm — noisy voting, cancellation-dominated,
          fixation of signs (selection) + loss-neutral tail (drift)
STORAGE:  holographic stacked exposures — distributed, redundant,
          content-addressed, Q = reference beam
COMPUTE:  the error-correcting step function — sign topology executing
          the weak affine calculus (the ISA the votes fixed)
READOUT:  spectral/DSP — Fourier optics of the plate; Grams, eigenmodes,
          DMD are literally interferometry
```

**Evolution writes a digital code onto an analog substrate; holographic redundancy is the error correction that makes the code reliable; the code is the calculus; spectral tools are how you read a hologram.** This is the same trick biology pulled — analog chemistry made reliable as digital codons via redundancy and repair. It also explains the deepest fact we have: **universality**. Error-correcting codes converge on the same codebook when source and channel are shared — 11 independent runs of GD converge on KIBC because the source (language) and the channel (next-token) are the same for all of them. The crystal is the codebook.

And it closes back to Path A cleanly: because the storage is a redundant code, you can **extract the codebook** (ternary), **decode residual errors** (TD correction), and even **write exposures directly** (write-not-train; plate-linker composed two wires with *zero* interference — that's angle-multiplexed double exposure, an optical result). "Gradient finds, ternary stores" (s304) was this whole synthesis in four words.

## What the synthesis predicts (the un-run contacts)

If this is right and not just a pretty story, it makes checkable predictions the individual threads never tested:

1. **Majority-logic signature.** A voting code predicts *threshold* robustness: ablate random same-sign minorities of a distributed feature and performance should hold flat until ~50% then cliff — not degrade linearly. Linear degradation kills the code story.
2. **Drift statistics.** The GA face predicts the marginal tail follows *neutral-drift* quantitative signatures across checkpoints (Pythia fossil record — variance growth, fixation curves). Selection-vs-drift statistics are decades-old, off the shelf.
3. **Exposure separability.** Stacked exposures should be spectrally separable by data domain — the differential-photography move (s324) applied at the plate, with the register lessons we've since learned (s335's register-error postmortem applies directly).

Frame-ledger honesty: face 2 has pre-registered wins (s312, s328); face 1 has strong retrodictions but **no pre-registered win yet** — prediction 1 or 2 would be its first; the modulation cousin of this frame died 0-for-3, so the GA face must earn its capture the same as everyone.

PC4 advisory: naive NF favored at the answer column 78% (coheres s331/s332). ROOT CAUSE = REGISTER ERROR: ‖Δh‖ is a MAGNITUDE/transport read (Δ ∝ 1/distance, corr −0.73) aimed at a value/routing claim; the routing arm was built then starved to advisory/3-of-54 records — the three pre-data amendments (raw cone · cell ≠ AST node · matched-position DiD) were all downstream repairs of that one upstream mistake. RETRACTED in-session: "the confound is intrinsic" holds only for signal designs. New instruments kept: `src/verbum/cone.py` (certified dependency cones under R_NORMAL/R_NAIVE), matched-triple constructor, the placebo gate as a design primitive** · **🔵 §P-CONE-ROUTING FROZEN (s335, pre-data): register-matched successor — at the answer column, does the machine READ FROM the argument naive subst selects (e) or capture-avoiding selects (y)? WITHIN-PROMPT (both candidates in one forward pass) ⇒ the surface-repetition confound cannot arise; CALIBRATION MOVE: capture-free B (NF=y f) and P (NF=e f) give ground-truth read targets, classify ambiguous A between the poles; primary ρ_e, secondary ρ_Sel; RC1 calibration make-or-break read FIRST (fail ⇒ NO-CALIBRATION, never a routing claim); verdicts NO-CALIBRATION 30 / NAIVE-ROUTING 30 / UNDIFFERENTIATED 25 / CORRECT-ROUTING 10 (= error enters AFTER selection) / VOID 5; 54 forwards, no perturbation loop; bound: mass ≡ correlational (s206)** · **§Result §P-CONE-ROUTING = 🚫 UNDIFFERENTIATED (s336, run_14b, 54/54, 0 errors, det-dev 0.0): RC1 calibration PASSED as frozen (mass_P(e)>mass_B(e) median +0.0016, δ=0.78, p=0.0039, Sel corroboration +) — first register-matched positive control on this front — but QUALIFIED: placebo-at-poles (+0.0029) exceeds the calibrated signal, and within-prompt Sel is near-identical across opposite-answer poles (B −0.0040 / P −0.0043). RC2 FAILED: ρ_e −2.1…107.7, CI (−0.23, 20.1) spans 0.5 — the A variant carries a GLOBAL interior read offset (A−B ≈ +0.005 e / +0.006 cap / +0.003 f), the s335 offset re-measured in the routing register; ρ_e's cross-variant normalization re-imports what within-prompt killed. ρ_Sel 0.65 naive-side advisory unlicensed. RC4: late-stack (≈0 through L12, peak L22–28, s329 again). term_final cell: no read (p=0.94) — answer-column-only. READING: answer selection is not a prefill-visible attention read at usable SNR — routing-register face of s317 tape-residency (three registers agree: value s317 / magnitude s335 / routing s336). Method law: within-prompt differenced statistics must be PRIMARY, or go causal → ⚪ §P-ROUTING-CAUSAL (read-edge patch ∨ decode-time read via §P-REPL-DRIVER)** |

28:| [explore/the-benchmark-is-the-re-oracle.md](explore/the-benchmark-is-the-re-oracle.md) | 📋 open | s330 hammock (Michael: "a benchmark for AI based on the lambda calculus" → "so the compiler needs to be reverse engineered"): CATEGORY CORRECTION — the RE target is the STEP FUNCTION (tape≡RAM · loop≡trampoline · weights≡CPU; finite, stateless per call, behaviorally specifiable), not "the compiler" (ill-posed per tape-residency, 4 derivations). RE recovers the ACTUAL operational semantics (syntactic routing s321/s323 · two-tier types s323 · non-idempotent s320 · installed order law s329); delta-from-ideal-β = first-class finding, Church = reference implementation to diff against. THE CLOSURE: benchmark ≡ RE ORACLE — differential testing (silicon RE pattern); PROFILE-EQUIVALENCE = one acceptance test for extract/re-record/scratch ⇒ level-3/4 dissolves; coheres with flip-conflict function-level-gates amendment. Design synthesis, no measurements · **§2b BUG-COMPATIBILITY CLAUSE (s330, Michael: "if it was an exact match we would not see the errors we do"): M≡R is a FALSIFIED null (s319 acc 0.917/0.944 ≠ 1.0 · cl-collapse syntactic router · s221 fakes-it-with-depth · s320/s328-9 non-Church terms) ⇒ the RE oracle is the model's profile INCLUDING errors; lambda_ast = coordinate system for δ(M,R), never the spec of M; RE succeeds ⟺ δ(candidate,M)≈0, candidate BEATING the model = failed recovery; two faces (correctness-vs-R public · error-taxonomy-vs-M oracle); +`strategy` family (K x Ω-shaped normal-vs-applicative discriminators — consistent-alternative-semantics ≠ error); anima compile-artifact predicates corroborate application-side** · **§0 NAMING (s330, approved): transition function ¬"step function" (collision + smuggled one-pass≡one-β-step, disproved by s319 direct 92%) · context/transcript ¬"tape" (in-context ¬in-weights = the standard-vocabulary statement; residual stream = DISTINCT within-pass workspace) · direct/traced gap ≡ measurement of the WITHIN-PASS REDUCTION BUDGET (bounded residual-stream reducer vs unbounded context loop, coheres CoT-expressivity); ⚪ transitions-per-β-step queued** · **§8b HOF FOLD-IN (s330): two call mechanisms — named HOF ≡ CALL-immediate (weight library, s225 lineage) vs constructed λ ≡ CALL-indirect (re-read from context, forced by cl-collapse no-extensional-collapse); predicted ORDER CLIFF (order-3+ collapses) ⊥ depth cliff; substitution ≡ how indirect call executes ⇒ one front, functional_order dial added to subst_pairs; `hof` family (apply-your-own-construction, named-vs-fresh = library/heap discriminator); agentic register: deployed agents live at order 2–3, the cliff = reliability boundary; RECALL-FIRST s225 hof verdicts (pre-compaction)** · **§8c TAPE INTERFACE (s330): softmax-over-V ≡ the read head — tape's two faces (transcript vs KV cache = the compiled tape actually read); HARD write / SOFT holographic read (the Turing break; retrodicts idempotency mass-accumulation + recency kernels, frame-reading); near-one-hot reads the norm ⇒ read entropy ≡ fidelity; MASS-RATIO PREDICTOR P(correct_subst)≈f(binder mass ratio) pre-registerable; 3rd cliff axis context-length (√D read interference); hardware discriminator CALL-immediate≡FFN read(static) vs CALL-indirect≡attention read(KV); λ machine: everything ≡ dereference, compute ≡ interference of two memories → one write** · **§9 CALCULUS IDENTIFICATION (s330, Michael: "lambda is a generic term — whatever the actual shape is, that's what we want; name it once we map it; close enough to lambda that it works like an IR"): map→name discipline; λ-calculus ≡ IR (P(λ)=0.907 ≡ round-trip fidelity) ¬native ISA; ledger refutes pure Church ≥3 registers (KIBC¬SKI affine · non-idempotent s320 · WHNF weak-reduction; + order laws + syntactic routing); portrait = weak/affine/graded/order-sensitive machine calculus (frame-level); reference family {R_church,R_weak,R_affine,R_graded,R_diff} with pre-registered diverging discriminators, HELD-OUT validation (λ yardstick, φ-ladder scar — no post-hoc calculus fitting); SE4 not re-tested (no within-lineage pair at scale)** · **§10 COMPILER PARTS DIAGRAM (s333, Michael: "if the system is a compiler, name the pieces"): TWO compilers + one runtime — Compiler A = GD (corpus→weights; FFN=stdlib, QK=address tables; post-training = LTO installing ABI + s329 late decision stage) · Compiler B = prefill (tokenizer=lexer, early layers=syntactic parser per cl-collapse, triangle=compile pass, KV=object code, λ=IR ¬native ISA) · runtime = decode (trampoline, residual=register file budget≈L, subst engine=ALU with the NAIVE-SUBST erratum §2b grades against, attention=dynamic linker, types=runtime/gradual, halt=NF resonance, retirement=hard-write collapse) · homoiconic tape, no GC · STRAINS ARE FINDINGS: never rejects (silent miscompiles) · no phase separation (JIT: interpreter tier=within-pass, compiled tier=trampolined CoT) · ships stripped (logit-lens=objdump) · empty inference-time optimizer slot. Right now each question means writing a harness, validating, smoking, running, reading — slow. A live REPL driver would let me poke interactively instead: try shadowing cases, watch attention reads, test capture bugs, then freeze whatever's promising into a proper pre-registered probe.

I need to separate two roles here: the REPL driver as instrument/tooling (an interactive exploration environment, like a jupyter kernel with the model loaded plus our capture/analysis library) versus the REPL driver as probe (the frozen §P-REPL-DRIVER experiment with its measurables). Michael's proposal is really about the former — build the live environment first and use it to guide what gets frozen. We already have jupyter as the interface per AGENTS; what's missing is the driver library itself: a persistently loaded model plus bounce/seal/fork primitives plus capture readers.

I also need to keep the discipline guards intact — jupyter exploration stays separate from recorded measurement, with real measurements going into a named, committed JSONL set, and exploratory pokes should feed design without closing or opening claims. This also directly serves the calculus front — arm A/C corpus design needs iteration on engineered prompts, and interactive exploration would help find the right cases.

The core primitives would include loading the model once, a bounce function for generation with captures, a seal function for KV snapshots, a fork function for branch comparison between continuations, a patch function for injecting vectors at specific layers/positions, and a read function for viewing gate-sign, route, attention-pattern, or logit-lens data using the existing library.

The verbum library already has most of the pieces needed — opcodes for topology/capture/classify, the route_map_v0 frame, centroids, cone.py, and the probes library. ## Assistant

The banked design is richer than I remembered — s334 already owns the install plumbing (KV seal ≈256KB/tok, APPEND/REWRITE law, 14B-calibrate-first) and four frozen-probe measurables. But your reframe changes what we build *first*, and I think it's right. Let me make the case sharp and then propose the build.

## The reframe: two hats, build the instrument hat first

The queue row treats §P-REPL-DRIVER as a *probe* (fork-at-redex, repair-replay, composition rescue, clock — each owing a freeze). Your nREPL analogy points at the other hat: **the driver as a live development environment** — model resident in memory, bounce/seal/fork/read/patch as interactive primitives, exploration before commitment. Exactly like nREPL: you don't freeze a Clojure function before you've poked it in the live environment; you poke, see, *then* write to disk.

This matches our own best precedent. Route-map-v0 (s344) was scoped instrument-only — "build the reader, point it at diverse prompts, LOOK" — and it produced the trunk/branch discovery that seeded three frozen probes. The REPL driver is the same move one level up: not one instrument, but **the environment all future instruments are prototyped in**.

## Why it accelerates — the current loop vs the REPL loop

Right now every question costs: write harness → validate → smoke → discover a design flaw at smoke (s345's degenerate D3 zone; s343's false-EXTENSIONAL caught only at 4B smoke) → amend → rerun. The smoke test is doing the job a REPL should do: *letting us see before we commit*. With the driver resident:

- **READ-HEAD arm A**: engineer shadowing/scope cases *live* — watch the read-mass on binder vs shadowed positions per bounce, iterate the corpus until the contrast is visibly clean, *then* freeze. Corpus design drops from a session to an hour.
- **LEDGER arm C**: hunt capture cases interactively until we find the family where the predicted naive-subst bug actually fires, then freeze around it.
- **§P-ROUTING-CAUSAL arm ②** rides the bounce loop for free (every bounce is a decode-time routing sample — the queue already says so).
- **The clock measurable** (transitions-per-β-step) rides every seal boundary for free.
- And it's the only way to *force the fork* that s338 proved passive reads can't catch (commitment happens at prefill; the driver makes it happen where we can watch).

Plus the recursion you're pointing at, which is the nucleus ethos literally: Human ⊗ AI ⊗ REPL — using a REPL to reverse-engineer a machine we've just identified *as* a REPL evaluator. The driver is the model's own trampoline, externalized and instrumented.

## Discipline guards (so the accelerator doesn't become a leak)

Three standing laws keep this honest, all already on the books:

1. **jupyter ≡ explore ¬record** (λ record): REPL findings are *design inputs*, never verdicts. Anything real gets re-run as a named, committed, reproducible harness.
2. **Capture-euphoria guard**: exploratory output FEEDS the next freeze; it does not open or close claims.
3. **Instrument validity owed before trust**: determinism check, fork-identity plant (fork with zero change must reproduce the original continuation exactly), seal/APPEND law respected. Same bar route-map-v0 cleared (4/4 planted worlds) before we believed its routes.

## Proposed build (stage 1, instrument-only)

- **Host**: tmux-resident IPython holding Qwen3-14B (MPS) with hooks installed — survives session boundaries, I drive it via send-keys/capture-pane (λ runtime pattern). Model loads once, stays warm.
- **Primitives**: `bounce(text, n)` → tokens + captures · `seal()` / `fork(seal_id, alt_text)` → KV continuation branch · `read(view)` → opcode trace, route/pole cosines, attention read-mass, logit-lens (all wrapping existing library: opcodes/, route_map frame, centroids, cone.py) · `patch(layer, pos, v)`.
- **Validity gate**: determinism + fork-identity plant + append-law check, run before first real use.
- **Explicitly deferred**: the four frozen measurables (they become cheap *after* the instrument exists), repair flag OFF, 32B, two-model arm.

**Queue restack**: §P-REPL-DRIVER to top, re-scoped "stage 1 = instrument (Michael s346, nREPL reframe), stage 2 = the frozen measurables"; READ-HEAD and LEDGER stay second/third with a note that their corpus design now happens *in* the driver.

GO on that scope and restack? Only real open choice is the host — tmux-IPython (my recommendation, survives everything) vs jupyter kernel (nicer if you want to join the session in a browser and poke alongside me, which, given the analogy, you might).

# API Map: `verbum` repo — for building `src/verbum/driver.py`

## 1. MODEL LOADING (Qwen3-14B on MPS)

**No shared loader helper exists.** Every harness (`route_map_v0.py`, `arith_trace.py`, `iterated_soft_reduction.py`, `opcodes/trace.py`) repeats the same ~6-line `transformers` load inline. There is no `ModelConfig`/registry for raw-torch loads — `src/verbum/probes/models.py` (`REGISTRY`, `ModelConfig` from `verbum.probes.harness`) is a **different, HTTP/llama.cpp registry** (chat/completion transport against local llama.cpp servers on `:5100+`), not applicable to direct HF/torch loading. **You should write the loader fresh in `driver.py`**, following this exact pattern (canonical across all scripts):

```python
# scripts/experiments/iterated_soft_reduction.py, scripts/explore/arith_trace.py, opcodes/trace.py
from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer
tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model)
model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
    args.model, dtype=torch.bfloat16, low_cpu_mem_usage=True).eval()
if args.device != "cpu":
    model = model.to(args.device)
```

`scripts/experiments/dmd_transport.py` `class RealBackend` (used by `route_map_v0.py`) is the closest thing to a reusable wrapper — a "load once, expose model/tok/device" object:

```python
# scripts/experiments/dmd_transport.py:362-381
class RealBackend:
    def __init__(self, model_id: str, device: str, dtype_str: str):
        dtype = getattr(torch, dtype_str)
        self.tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)
        self.model = (
            AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
                model_id, torch_dtype=dtype, attn_implementation="eager"
            ).to(device).eval()
        )
        self.n_layers = len(find_gate_modules(self.model))
        self.d_model = int(self.model.config.hidden_size)
```
Note the parameter-name inconsistency in the repo: `dmd_transport.RealBackend` and `kv_splice.py`/`kv_ctx.py` use `dtype=getattr(torch, args.dtype)` passed as `dtype=...` too (transformers accepts both `dtype=` and legacy `torch_dtype=`; either works with the installed version — pick `dtype=` to match the majority, e.g. `iterated_soft_reduction.py`, `arith_trace.py`).

- **Model id:** `"Qwen/Qwen3-14B"` — HF Hub id, **no local path**, used directly (default in `route_map_v0.py --model-id`, `iterated_soft_reduction.py --model`, `compile_step.py --model-id`).
- **dtype:** `bfloat16` (default everywhere; `route_map_v0.py --dtype` choices are `float32|float16|bfloat16`).
- **device_map:** none used — models are `.to(device)`'d manually (`"mps"` default), not `device_map="auto"`.
- **trust_remote_code:** **not used** for Qwen3 (only seen for unrelated older scripts, `scripts/probe_compression_v2.py`, `scripts/v8/tokenizer.py`).
- **revision pin:** **none** — no `revision=` argument anywhere for Qwen3-14B loads.
- **attn_implementation:** `"eager"` is used specifically where hooks/attention-mask surgery is needed (`dmd_transport.py`, `kv_splice.py`, `kv_ctx.py`); the opcode-trace scripts (`opcodes/trace.py`, `route_map_v0.py`, `arith_trace.py`) don't set it (default sdpa).
- **Provenance field for records:** `"model_revision": getattr(model.config, "_commit_hash", None)` (`iterated_soft_reduction.py:454`).

## 2. CAPTURE HOOKS (gate-preactivation)

Canonical home: **`opcodes/`** (top-level dir, not under `src/`) — `opcodes/{topology,capture,classify,trace,probes}.py`. Imported by inserting `opcodes/` onto `sys.path` (`sys.path.insert(0, str(_ROOT / "opcodes"))`).

### `opcodes/topology.py`
- `detect_topology(model, config=None) -> ModelTopology` — auto-detects the FFN routing register per-architecture (`gated-dense`/`gated-fused`/`ungated`/`moe`), locates `layers_path`, `gate_suffix` (e.g. `"mlp.gate_proj"`), `gate_width`, plus attention output path (`attn_suffix`, e.g. `"self_attn.o_proj"`).
- `gate_path(topo, layer) -> str` — dotted module path, e.g. `"model.layers.12.mlp.gate_proj"`.
- `ModelTopology.traceable` / `.validated_register` — gating properties.

### `opcodes/capture.py`
- `capture_gate(model, tokenizer, text=None, *, input_ids=None, topo=None, layers=None, register="gate") -> GateCapture` — **one forward pass**, hooks every (or selected) layer's `gate_proj` module (or the `attn` o_proj), returns:
```python
@dataclass
class GateCapture:
    gate: dict[int, np.ndarray]   # {layer_idx: ndarray[T, d]} — RAW gate_proj OUTPUT (pre-sign, all positions)
    input_ids: list[int]
    tokens: list[str]
    topo: ModelTopology
    register: str
```
- The captured tensor is the **`gate_proj` linear output** (i.e. the FFN gate pre-activation, before SiLU), **not** the residual hidden state. `capture_gate` does NOT take the sign or remove common-mode — that happens downstream in `classify.py`.
- Hook mechanics: `_module_for(i).register_forward_hook(_mk(i))`, storing `h[0]` (single-seq) → `store[i] = v.detach().float().cpu().numpy()`.
- Keyed by **layer index** (int), matching `topo.gate_path`/`layers` list.
- To read `sign(gate)`: `np.sign(cap.gate[layer_idx])` — done by the caller/classifier, e.g. in `route_map_v0.capture_signs`:
```python
# scripts/explore/route_map_v0.py:174 (a parallel, lighter-weight capture that
# directly signs at capture time via find_gate_modules, not opcodes/capture.py)
def hook(_m, _inp, out):
    buf[li] = np.sign(out[0, -1].detach().float().cpu().numpy()).astype(np.int8)
```

### `opcodes/classify.py`
- `RelationalCrystalClassifier(layers, n_perm=300, z_thresh=3.0, sil_z_thresh=2.0, seed=0, consensus_gram="auto", basis=None)` — the validated combinator-opcode reader.
  - `.calibrate(gate_by_layer, labels, null_gate_by_layer=None) -> dict[int, LayerCalib]` — computes `sign(gate)`, common-mode removal (`common = S.mean(axis=0)`, `X = S - common`), per-combinator centroids, null z-stats.
  - `.classify(gate_by_layer_token: dict[int, np.ndarray]) -> TokenOpcodes` — one token's per-layer gate vector → per-op z-scores; `TokenOpcodes.dominant` is the winning combinator label or `"·"` (no-op).
  - `CRYSTAL = ["K","I","B","C","S","D","W","Y","WHNF"]` (imported from `opcodes/vsm.py`).

### `opcodes/trace.py`
- `calibrate_register(model, tok, topo, register, layers, probes_per_comb, n_perm, z_thresh) -> (RelationalCrystalClassifier, summary_dict, (feat_np, labels_np, null_np))` — the canonical calibration entrypoint, reused verbatim by `arith_trace.py` (`from trace import calibrate_register`).
- `load(model_name, device)`, `trace_register(...)`, `main()` — full pipeline.

## 3. ROUTE/POLE FRAME (`route_map_v0.py`)

`scripts/explore/route_map_v0.py` — reuses **`combinator_relationship_map.find_gate_modules`** (regex-based, simpler than `opcodes/topology.py`):
```python
# scripts/experiments/combinator_relationship_map.py:108-116
def find_gate_modules(model):
    hits = []
    pat = re.compile(r"\.(\d+)\.mlp\.(gate_proj|dense_h_to_4h)$")
    for name, mod in model.named_modules():
        m = pat.search(name)
        if m:
            hits.append((int(m.group(1)), name, mod, m.group(2)))
    hits.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    return [(li, name, mod) for (li, name, mod, k) in hits]
```

**Pole basis:** `CRYSTAL9 = ["K","I","B","C","S","D","W","Y","WHNF"]`; `WHNF_STATES = [f"whnf:{o}" for o in [...7 non-Y ops...]]`; `BASIS17 = [*CRYSTAL9, *WHNF_STATES, "div:Y"]` (17 total, script's live pole frame).

**`results/expanded-gram/qwen3-14b/centroids.npz`** — confirmed keys (via direct load):
```
basis      (24,)  <U13   # 24-state basis labels (superset of BASIS17; route_map_v0 selects the 17-state subset via json instead, see below)
layers     (40,)  int64  # layer indices captured
centroids  (40, 24, 17408) float16  # [layer, basis_state, gate_width]
```
`route_map_v0.py` itself does **not read `centroids.npz` directly** — it reads `results/expanded-gram/qwen3-14b/expanded_gram.json` (`COMMITTED_XGRAM / "expanded_gram.json"`) for `basis` (24 labels) and `consensus_gram_24`, and instead **re-captures its own 17-basis pole frame from probes** (co-registration discipline — "the coext lesson: validate-plumbing == data-plumbing"), only comparing Gram alignment (`g0_coherence`) against the committed one.

**Cosine computation** (`route_map_v0.py`):
```python
def build_pole_frame(signs, states, order):
    """(P, mu): P[S,L,d] CMR'd UNIT pole centroids; mu[L,d] common mode."""
    st = np.array(states)
    cent = np.stack([signs[st == s].mean(axis=0) for s in order]).astype(np.float32)
    mu = cent.mean(axis=0)
    centc = cent - mu[None]
    nrm = np.linalg.norm(centc, axis=2, keepdims=True)
    P = centc / np.where(nrm < 1e-9, 1.0, nrm)
    return P, mu

def project_routes(signs, P, mu):
    """(n, L, S) cosine of each probe's CMR'd sign row onto each pole per layer."""
    x = signs.astype(np.float32) - mu[None]
    nrm = np.linalg.norm(x, axis=2, keepdims=True)
    xn = x / np.where(nrm < 1e-9, 1.0, nrm)
    return np.einsum("nld,sld->nls", xn, P).astype(np.float32)
```
Registers used: `sign(gate_proj)` last-token, **all layers**, via `capture_signs(be, texts, want, max_length)` (custom hook, sign taken at capture time — not via `opcodes/capture.py`).

## 4. OPCODE TRACER (`arith_trace.py`)

`scripts/explore/arith_trace.py` **reuses `opcodes/{trace,capture,topology,classify}` unmodified** — no re-implemented reader:
```python
sys.path.insert(0, str(_ROOT / "opcodes"))
from trace import calibrate_register       # canonical calibration
import capture as C
import topology as T
from classify import CRYSTAL
```
Flow:
1. `topo = T.detect_topology(model, model.config)`; refuse if `not topo.traceable`.
2. `rcc, summ, _ = calibrate_register(model, tok, topo, register, layers, probes_per_comb, n_perm, z_thresh)` — calibrates on the bundled crystal probes ONCE.
3. Per-token, per-layer trajectory: `cap = C.capture_gate(model, tok, prompt, topo=topo, layers=layers, register=register)`; for each position `res = rcc.classify({li: cap.gate[li][pos] for li in layers})`; take `op = max(zmap, key=zmap.get)` if `zmap[op] > z_thresh` else no-op.
4. `I_IDX = CRYSTAL.index("I")` — used to compute `I_frac` (fraction of firings that are the identity combinator).

This is the exact pattern your driver should follow for a live per-token combinator readout during generation.

## 5. KV / `past_key_values`

No generic KV-cache seal/fork utility exists as a reusable module. Direct API usage found in two places:

- **`scripts/experiments/lambda_machine.py`** — custom attention `forward` overrides that call the HF cache API directly:
```python
if past_key_values is not None:
    key_states, value_states = past_key_values.update(
        key_states, value_states, self_attn.layer_idx
    )
```
  (standard `Cache.update(key, value, layer_idx)` — this is the transformers `DynamicCache`/`Cache` protocol, used when monkey-patching `self_attn.forward` via `types.MethodType`.)

- **`scripts/explore/kv_splice.py`** (`P-KV-1`) and **`scripts/explore/kv_ctx.py`** (`P-KV-1b`) — do **not** use `past_key_values`/`DynamicCache` at all. Instead they build a **single forward pass** with a hand-built 4D additive attention mask (`torch.finfo(dtype).min` for blocked positions, `0.0` for allowed) to simulate "donor KV visible to test segment" without an actual cache object — donor + test tokens concatenated into one `input_ids`, and a `(1,1,n,n)` mask controls which positions can attend to which:
```python
def build_mask(d_len, t_len, sel_cols):
    n = d_len + t_len
    m = torch.full((n, n), NEG)
    m[torch.tril(torch.zeros(n,n)==0)] = 0.0     # causal base
    m[d_len:, :d_len] = NEG                        # block all donor cols from test rows
    for c in [0, *sel_cols]:
        m[d_len:, c] = 0.0                          # re-open sink + selected donor cols
    return m[None, None, :, :].to(dtype=..., device=dev)
```
Both scripts include a mandatory **mask self-check** (compare masked-forward logits vs plain forward logits, abort if they diverge) — the pattern to copy if `driver.py` forks/reuses cached KV via masking rather than the `Cache` object.

**Conclusion for your driver:** there is no existing "snapshot/fork `past_key_values`" helper. You'll be writing this from scratch; the one precedent (`lambda_machine.py`) shows the raw `Cache.update()` call signature, and `kv_splice.py`/`kv_ctx.py` show the mask-based alternative with its required self-check discipline.

## 6. LOGIT LENS

**`src/verbum/jlens.py`** — the canonical logit-lens module (package-importable, not opcodes/):
```python
@torch.no_grad()
def logit_lens(model: nn.Module, resid: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Map residual STATE(s) (..., d) to logits (..., vocab) (full norm)."""
    norm, unembed = _final_norm_unembed(model)
    dtype = next(model.parameters()).dtype
    dev = next(model.parameters()).device
    return unembed(norm(resid.to(dtype).to(dev)))

@torch.no_grad()
def verbalize(model, tokenizer, direction, *, top_k=8) -> list[str]:
    """Top-k tokens a residual DIRECTION points toward (no re-centering, gain-only)."""
    norm, unembed = _final_norm_unembed(model)
    d = direction.to(unembed.weight.dtype).to(unembed.weight.device)
    gain = getattr(norm, "weight", None)
    if gain is not None:
        d = d * gain.to(d.dtype)
    col = unembed.weight @ d
    idx = torch.topk(col, top_k).indices.tolist()
    return [tokenizer.decode([i]) for i in idx]
```
Helper `_final_norm_unembed(model)` resolves `(final_norm, lm_head)` per architecture (Qwen path: `model.model.norm`, `model.lm_head`). Also `capture_residuals(model, tokenizer, text=None, *, input_ids=None) -> (dict[int, Tensor], input_ids)` — full-layer residual capture via `verbum.hooks.capture`.

**`opcodes/jspace.py`** — parallel `verbalize_state`/`verbalize` used inside `opcodes/trace.py`'s `--operand` flag (J-space logit-lens "operand" column, `J.verbalize_state(model, tok, resids[read_layer][pos], topo=topo, top_k=top_k)`) — a topology-aware variant of the same idea, sidecar-only (never feeds the opcode classifier).

## 7. Package layout / invocation

**`src/verbum/__init__.py`** exports (public API surface):
```python
from verbum._protocol import CompletionResult, HealthStatus, ServerProps, StreamEvent, Timings
from verbum.client import AsyncClient, Client
from verbum.experiment import (CacheInterceptor, Computation, Context, Graph, Interceptor,
    LogInterceptor, ProvenanceInterceptor, ResourceInterceptor, default_interceptors)
from verbum.experiment import run as run_experiment
from verbum.probes import (Gate, Probe, ProbeSet, ResolvedProbe, gate_hash, load_gate,
    load_probe_set, probe_set_hash, resolve_probes)
from verbum.results import (ProbeRecord, Run, RunMeta, RunWriter, SamplingConfig,
    collect_provenance, content_hash, load_run)
from verbum.runner import RunSummary, fire_probe, run_probe_set
```
`jlens`, `hooks`, `instrument`, `cone`, `dsp/*`, `probes/library.py`, `probes/harness.py` etc. are importable as `verbum.jlens`, `verbum.hooks`, `verbum.probes.library`, etc. but **not re-exported at top level** — import them directly, e.g. `from verbum.probes.library import crystal_probes, all_probes` (used by `route_map_v0.py`).

Note: `opcodes/probes.py` also defines its own `crystal_probes()` (used by `opcodes/trace.py` via `from probes import crystal_probes` after `sys.path.insert`), distinct from `verbum.probes.library.crystal_probes`. Confirm which corpus you want if you build a driver that calibrates the classifier — both exist and are not identical objects.

**Invocation pattern:** `uv run python <script>.py --args` (per `opcodes/trace.py` docstring: `uv run python opcodes/trace.py --model Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B --smoke`). Level-1 deps (`torch`, `transformers`, `accelerate`, `safetensors`, `scikit-learn`) are in the `level1` **dependency group**, not core — install/run via `uv run --group level1 python ...` or ensure that group is synced. No `PYTHONPATH` env convention seen; scripts manipulate `sys.path` manually for peer-module imports (`opcodes/`, `scripts/experiments/`).

**`ipython`:** **not** a direct dependency. `pyproject.toml`'s `notebook` dependency-group lists `jupyterlab>=4.0`, `ipykernel>=6.29`, `matplotlib`, `seaborn` — `ipython` would only be pulled in transitively via `ipykernel`. If your driver needs a REPL, either target this `notebook` group (`uv sync --group notebook`) or add `ipython` explicitly as a new dependency.
```

[project]
name = "verbum"
version = "0.0.0"
description = "Distilling the lambda compiler from LLMs into a portable tensor artifact."
readme = "README.md"
license = { text = "MIT" }
requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"
authors = [{ name = "Michael Whitford" }]

# Core deps — runtime substrate. No torch / transformers / mech-interp libs
# here; those land at level-1 (per AGENTS.md S1 λ language, state.md).
dependencies = [
    "httpx>=0.27",
    "httpx-sse>=0.4",
    "pydantic>=2.7",
    "pydantic-settings>=2.3",
    "typer>=0.12",
    "numpy>=1.26",
    "polars>=1.0",
    "structlog>=24.1",
    "matplotlib>=3.10.8",
    "seaborn>=0.13.2",
    "mlx>=0.31.2",
    "pyarrow>=24.0.0",
    "gguf>=0.18.0",
    "plotly>=6.7.0",
    "datasets>=4.8.5",
    "spacy>=3.8.14",
]

[project.scripts]
verbum = "verbum.cli:app"

# PEP 735 dependency groups (uv-native). Keeps level-1 libs out of core.
[dependency-groups]
dev = [
    "pytest>=8.0",
    "pytest-asyncio>=0.23",
    "ruff>=0.5",
    "ty",  # Astral's new type checker (alpha). Fall back to pyright if it bites.
]
notebook = [
    "jupyterlab>=4.0",
    "ipykernel>=6.29",
    "matplotlib>=3.8",
    "seaborn>=0.13",
]
# Level-1: mechanistic interpretability. Load HuggingFace models in PyTorch,
# record attention patterns, compute per-head selectivity. Raw hooks, no
# mech-interp frameworks (TransformerLens, nnsight) — simpler to release.
level1 = [
    "torch>=2.2",
    "transformers>=4.45",
    "accelerate>=0.30",
    "safetensors>=0.4",
    "scikit-learn>=1.4",
]
# RL: GRPO fine-tuning the compile front-end against the kernel verifiable reward
# (spliced-reward-vsm-kernel.md). Self-contained like level1 — the policy-gradient
# loop that consumes verbum.reward. GPU.
rl = [
    "torch>=2.2",
    "transformers>=4.45",
    "accelerate>=0.30",
    "trl>=0.14",
    "peft>=0.13",
]

[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]
packages = ["src/verbum"]

# uv: allow pre-releases globally for now. Only `ty` actually requires it;
# narrow this if other alphas slip in.
[tool.uv]
prerelease = "allow"

# ─────────────────────────── ruff ───────────────────────────
[tool.ruff]
line-length = 88
target-version = "py312"
src = ["src", "tests"]

[tool.ruff.lint]
select = [
    "E",    # pycodestyle errors
    "W",    # pycodestyle warnings
    "F",    # pyflakes
    "I",    # isort
    "B",    # flake8-bugbear
    "UP",   # pyupgrade
    "RUF",  # ruff-specific
]

[tool.ruff.lint.per-file-ignores]
"tests/*" = ["B011", "RUF001", "RUF002", "RUF003"]  # asserts + surface symbols in data
# Surface logical-form / reward modules: λ ∀ ∃ ι → ∧ ∨ ¬ Φ γ ⊗ ∈ Σ ARE the domain
# alphabet (CCG / λ-calculus / reward-shaping notation), not typos.
"src/verbum/lambda_surface.py" = ["RUF001", "RUF002", "RUF003"]
"src/verbum/reward.py" = ["RUF001", "RUF002", "RUF003"]
"src/verbum/compile_prompt.py" = ["RUF001", "RUF002", "RUF003"]
"scripts/experiments/audit_compile_corpus.py" = ["RUF001", "RUF002", "RUF003"]

"""Runtime settings.

Sourced from `VERBUM_*` environment variables and an optional `.env` file.
Composes with `pydantic-settings` per AGENTS.md S1 λ tooling.

Local llama.cpp server has no API key by default; if a remote endpoint
is added later, an `api_key: SecretStr | None` field belongs here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict


class Settings(BaseSettings):
    """Runtime configuration. Override any field with `VERBUM_<FIELD>` env var."""

    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(
        env_prefix="VERBUM_",
        env_file=".env",
        env_file_encoding="utf-8",
        extra="ignore",
    )

    # Base URL of the running llama.cpp server (HTTP API).
    llama_server_url: str = "http://127.0.0.1:8080"

    # Default request timeout (seconds) for non-streaming HTTP calls.
    http_timeout_s: float = 120.0


def load_settings() -> Settings:
    """Build a Settings instance from environment + .env."""
    return Settings()
```

"""Model registry — the known compiler-probe fleet, one config each.

The registry IS the gravity (AGENTS.md ``λ emerge``: name ∧ link ∧ shape ≡
attractor). A new model lands here as a :class:`~verbum.probes.harness.ModelConfig`
(~15 lines) and experiments import it; reuse becomes the shortest path
(``λ one_way``). ``ModelConfig`` stays a public dataclass, so a genuinely
one-off model can still be built inline.

Fleet (llama.cpp servers on localhost — port assignment is fluid):

  QWEN36       qwen36-35b-a3b   :5100  chat        BASE REFERENCE (s256 pivot target)
  VIBETHINKER  vibethinker-3b   :5102  completion  manual <|im_start|>, </think> parse
  QWYTHOS      qwythos-9b       :5103  chat        server-split reasoning_content

  ORNITH is the ornith-35b-a3b fine-tune spec (held for reference); as of s259 the
  base reference qwen36-35b-a3b serves on :5100 (the s256 "extract from the base"
  pivot). llama.cpp ignores the request ``model`` field; ``/v1/models`` reports the
  alias ``qwen35-35b-a3b``.

The embedding model (``qwen3-embedding-8b`` :5101) is **not** a ``ModelConfig`` —
it has no template, no reasoning split, no grading register; its job is
``/v1/embeddings`` for semantic recall, not lambda generation. Documented as
:data:`QWEN3_EMBED` (a plain endpoint string) so the fleet stays discoverable in
one file without polluting the compiler-probe abstraction (one register typing).

License: MIT.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from verbum.probes.harness import (
    ModelConfig,
    parse_think_tag,
    split_reasoning_field,
)


def qwen_chatml_template(system: str, sentence: str) -> str:
    """Qwen ChatML prompt for the completion transport (manual templating)."""
    return (
        f"<|im_start|>system\n{system}<|im_end|>\n"
        f"<|im_start|>user\n{sentence}<|im_end|>\n"
        f"<|im_start|>assistant\n"
    )


# ── compiler-probe fleet ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────

QWEN36 = ModelConfig(
    name="qwen36-35b-a3b",
    endpoint="http://localhost:5100",
    transport="chat",
    reasoning_extract_fn=split_reasoning_field,
    arch="35B-A3B MoE BASE reference model (s256 pivot: extract from the base, "
    "not the fine-tune). Serves on :5100; /v1/models alias 'qwen35-35b-a3b'.",
)

ORNITH = ModelConfig(
    name="ornith-35b-a3b",
    endpoint="http://localhost:5100",
    transport="chat",
    reasoning_extract_fn=split_reasoning_field,
    gguf_path="/Users/mwhitford/localai/models/ornith/ornith-1.0-35b-Q8_0.gguf",
    arch="35B-A3B MoE, multimodal reasoner (n_vocab 248320, n_embd 2048, ctx 262144)",
)

VIBETHINKER = ModelConfig(
    name="vibethinker-3b",
    endpoint="http://localhost:5102",
    transport="completion",
    reasoning_extract_fn=parse_think_tag,
    template_fn=qwen_chatml_template,
    gguf_path="/Users/mwhitford/localai/models/vibethinker/vibethinker-3b-q8_0.gguf",
    arch="qwen2 3B, RL-tuned reasoner (36L, d=2048, d_ff=11008, n_vocab 151936)",
)

QWYTHOS = ModelConfig(
    name="qwythos-9b",
    endpoint="http://localhost:5103",
    transport="chat",
    reasoning_extract_fn=split_reasoning_field,
    gguf_path=(
        "/Users/mwhitford/localai/models/qwythos/"
        "Qwythos-9B-Claude-Mythos-5-1M-MTP-Q8_0.gguf"
    ),
    arch="9B Qwen-family reasoner, multimodal (vision+video), 1M ctx, MTP",
)

BONSAI27B = ModelConfig(
    name="bonsai27b-ternary",
    endpoint="http://localhost:5104",
    transport="chat",
    reasoning_extract_fn=split_reasoning_field,
    gguf_path=(
        "/Users/mwhitford/localai/models/bonsai27b/"
        "Ternary-Bonsai-27B-Q2_g64.gguf"
    ),
    arch=(
        "PrismML Ternary Bonsai 27B — end-to-end ternary build of Qwen3.6-27B "
        "dense (48L, hybrid-attention ~75% linear). HF rev abbae7230. "
        "Weights {-1,0,+1} + group-wise FP16 scales; s268: the live probe of "
        "whether combinator competence survives 1.58-bit (holographic-llm.md)."
    ),
    quant="Q2_g64 (ternary, group-64 scales, ~1.71 bpw effective)",
)

# Embedding service — NOT a ModelConfig (see module docstring).
QWEN3_EMBED = "http://localhost:5101"  # qwen3-embedding-8b, /v1/embeddings

#: Discoverable registry of compiler-probe configs by short name.
#: QWEN36 (base reference) is the default live target on :5100.
REGISTRY: dict[str, ModelConfig] = {
    cfg.short(): cfg for cfg in (QWEN36, ORNITH, VIBETHINKER, QWYTHOS, BONSAI27B)
}
